[6417] TOOLRESULT: 
- An actor access has `accessType` `ALL` or `DERIVED`. `ALL` is fixed to `0..*` and has no getter; `DERIVED` has a configurable cardinality (`0..1` or `0..*`) and a getter ([accesspoint.md](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/accesspoint.md), *AccessType Behaviour*).
- Getter rules, all at once ([architectural patterns](../../agent-docs/backend/architectural-patterns.md), *Rules for `accesses[*].getterExpression`*):
  - the filter starts from the entity, not from the transfer object
  - `self` is not in scope inside an actor access getter; iterate from the entity with a lambda variable
  - a `DERIVED` access needs an expression that returns references; for "see everything" use `ALL` with no getter
  - `!getVariable(...)` returns a scalar; compare with `==`, and qualify the primitive type
- Typical shapes:
  - status filter: `Event!filter(e | e.status == EventStatus#OPEN)`
  - singleton: an expression ending in `!any()` with cardinality `0..1`
  - ownership: compare an entity field to the actor's own value, for example `!getVariable("ACTOR", "email")`
  - role-scoped: one `!filter` whose lambda disjuncts a role flag and the ownership clause; JQL has no if/else ([advanced modeling patterns](../../agent-docs/model/advanced-modeling-patterns.md), *Role-scoped access getters*)
- Inside a derived attribute on the principal's own transfer object, `self` is the actor's row (used for flags such as `isNotAdmin`); that is a different place from the access getter.
- Admin actors common
[6418] TOOLRESULT: 
| State | Realm Group | Claims | Managed |
|-------|-------------|--------|---------|
| `isEffectiveAnonymous() = true` | AnonymousGroup shown | Hidden | Hidden |
| `isEffectiveAnonymous() = false` | RealmGroup shown | Visible | Visible |

**`isEffectiveAnonymous()` returns true when `anonymous = true` and the principal is not mapped:**
- `principal` is null: the `anonymous` flag decides.
- `principal` is set but NOT mapped: the `anonymous` flag decides.
- `principal` is mapped (`principal.mapping` is set): always false, whatever the flag. An actor with a mapped principal and `anonymous = true` is authenticated: it gets a realm, and its calls need a bearer token (401 without one).

- **Exposing an operation without login**: put the operation's transfer object (or an access to it) on an `ActorType` that is effectively anonymous (`anonymous = true` with no principal or an unmapped principal). Such an actor gets no `realm`, so `asm2keycloak` creates no realm or client for it, and the REST layer does not verify a bearer token for its paths: the call runs with no principal and no `ACTOR`. Everything reachable through that actor's accesses is public; keep them minimal and put guards in an interceptor or in the operation itself. *Verify:* access control applied to a call with no principal outside the Keycloak interceptor was not checked.
- **`isActiveExpression`**: a JQL expression on the principal entity (for example `self.active`, a boolean attribute). It is copied to the filter 
[6419] TOOLRESULT: 
Entities often have a stable machine code and a label people recognise. Flagging both as identifiers documents both as natural keys in the model.

The agent-docs state that the runtime checks each `identifier` attribute on its own, as a separate unique constraint, and never the combination ([structure.md](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/structure.md), *DataMember*). Enforce uniqueness of the combination with a computed stored attribute or in project code, and say so in the model's documentation.
[6420] TOOLRESULT: 
```
public/i18n/
  application_hu-HU.json    # model-derived translations
  system_hu-HU.json         # framework UI strings
  i18nCleaner.py            # remove unused keys
  sort_json.py              # sort keys
  update-localization.py    # merge newly generated keys with manual ones
```

The scripts are custom tools, not platform features. This setup keeps `application_hu-HU.json` and `system_hu-HU.json` in `.generator-ignore` and runs them after model changes.

- Translate enumerations including `_null`, menu labels and every entity field.
- Diff the key set against `application_default.json` to find untranslated keys after each model change.
- Keep project-specific strings under a `custom.*` prefix, see [[i18n-custom-translation-keys]].
- The layout default language can stay `en`; the Hungarian files supply all visible strings.
[6421] TOOLRESULT: 
The generated `application/integration-test/pom.xml` declares `hu.blackbelt.osgi.i18n:i18n-api` with `<version></version>`. The generator never sets the `osgiI18nApiVersion` template variable, so the value is empty and Maven fails to resolve the dependency. Before running the tests, set the version explicitly to the `i18n-api` version your platform release uses, then run the commands below.


Run All Integration Tests

```bash
mvn clean test
```


Run Specific Test Class

```bash
mvn clean test -Dtest=UserDaoTest
```


Run Specific Test Method

```bash
mvn clean test -Dtest=UserDaoTest#testCreateUser
```
[6422] TOOLRESULT: 
- An entity with no attributes or relations (named Application or Initializer) hosts static operations: an initializer that seeds data, registration, bulk actions. Operation type `INITIALIZER` is stored as a static operation with the initializer flag set ([operation reference](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/operation.md)). The platform calls every initializer operation (unbound, no parameters) at each start of the application, once the scripts are ready. A failing one is logged and the completion marker is not registered, so dependants that wait for it do not start. Guard the body so a later start changes nothing; see [[initializer-operation]] for the seeding practice.
- If clients should call its operations, expose the entity through the same single-instance Access. Several projects did not expose it at all: it exists only for startup seeding.
[6423] TOOLRESULT: 
Three distinct places an operation can surface in the UI:

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  O["operation::Operation<br/>on TransferObjectType"]
  OF["ui:OperationForm<br/>(widget / button)"]
  TO["ui:TableOperation<br/>(row / table action)"]
  MIO["ui:MenuItemOperation<br/>(menu entry)"]
  O -->|name| OF
  O -->|xmi:id| TO
  O -->|targetOperation| MIO
  TO -->|operationForm<br/>(nested)| OF
```

| Placement | When to use |
|---|---|
| `OperationForm` inside View/Form/Group | The operation sits inline in a page or dialog |
| `OperationForm` inside `ActionGroup` | Grouping related operations with an overflow menu |
| `TableOperation.operationForm` inside `rowOperations` | Per-row action (single record context) |
| `TableOperation.operationForm` inside `tableOperations` | Toolbar action (collection or selection context); set `isBulk=true` for multi-select |
| `MenuItemOperation` on ActorType | Static/global operation triggered from the top-level menu |

`confirmationType` on `OperationForm` controls dialogs:
- `NONE` — no confirmation
- `CONDITIONAL` — `confirmationCondition` (boolean `DataMember`) decides
- `MANDATORY` — always confirm, set `confirmationMessage`

**`confirmationCondition`.** An operation button with a confirmation asks before it runs. Without `confirmationCondition` it always asks. With it, set to a boolean `DataMember` on the owner transfer object, the dialog appears only when that attribute is true in the current data (the row for table buttons, the owner data othe
[6424] TOOLRESULT: - Two clocks disagree:
  - `Timestamp!now()` / `current_timestamp` in model expressions → platform `defaultZoneId` (`JUDO_PLATFORM_DEFAULT_ZONE_ID`; template default `UTC`), not JVM zone.
  - `LocalDateTime.now()` in custom Java → JVM `TZ` / `user.timezone`.
  - Persistence converts to UTC; JSON serializes ISO offset/UTC.
- Mixing clocks for write vs compare gives fixed offset that breaks age/duration limits. Use one clock for both.
- UI timestamps format in browser zone (`Intl.DateTimeFormat` without `timeZone`). Model-built strings use `defaultZoneId` → mismatch (e.g. 18:00 vs 20:00). Model cannot read browser zone: show the timestamp itself, avoid derived formatted strings.
[6425] TOOLRESULT: 
- **Update operation FQN**: `#_updateInstance[FullEntityFQN]`
- **Payload type check**: `instanceof UpdateInstanceCallPayload`
- **Batch processing**: Query and process all related entities
- **Cascading updates**: Propagate changes to dependent entities
- **Old versus new values**: `UpdateInstanceCallPayload.getInput()` holds the values sent by the client (the new values); `getInstance()` identifies the instance (`__identifier`) and does not carry its stored values. To compare, read the stored entity with the DAO in `preCall` (before the write) using the identifier from `getInstance()`, then compare it with `getInput()`.

[6432] TOOLRESULT: 
- Translations are flat files in `public/i18n/`: `application_<locale>.json` (generated from the model) and `system_<locale>.json` (platform UI), plus `*_default.json` fallbacks. Keys are flat dot-notation under a `translation` root ([directory structure](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md)).
- Generated key patterns: `entities.<Entity>.<Component>.<field>`, `enumerations.<Type>.<VALUE>` and `enumerations.<Type>._null`, `menuTree.<label>`, `services.<service>...`, `faults.<prefix>.<object>.<attribute>`, `extra.localization.operation.<name>` ([key conventions](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md)).
- The generator writes `application_default.json` from the model. The generated `sync-translations.py` merges it into `application_<defaultLanguage>.json`: it adds new model keys, keeps existing values, and drops keys no longer in the source. Other locale files, such as a script-written `application_hu-HU.json`, are not written by the generator and survive regeneration; `.generator-ignore` protects any file the generator does write, and once ignored it no longer follows the model ([best practices](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md), see [[generator-ignore-i18n-layout]]).
- Adding a language: copy the default files, translate, update the language selector. Users switch language at runtime through i18n `changeLanguage` and a registered language dropdown ([adding languages](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md)).

[6433] CALL kb_get: {"path": "best-practices/backend/injectable-business-clock.md", "section": "Practice > Practice notes: one clock, one override"}
[6434] TOOLRESULT: 
The classes below are example code, not platform API.

1. A clock holder: a final class with static accessors (`today()`, `now()`, `nowUtc()`, `instant()`, `get()` and a UTC variant). It has no OSGi dependency, so common services, custom operations and interceptors all use it the same way. An injected `java.time.Clock` field is the alternative when the caller is a component that tests can construct.
2. Production: no override, in the zone of the platform parameter `defaultZoneId` (default `UTC`), not `Clock.systemDefaultZone()`. Model `Timestamp!now()` uses that zone, so a JVM-zone clock is offset from model expressions whenever the two zones differ.
3. Test or demo: a system property or an environment variable holds an ISO local date-time (a bare date means start of day). It is read once at class load.
4. The overridden clock is an offset clock: it starts at the chosen instant and keeps advancing, so timers and audit ordering still work. It does not freeze time.
5. A value that cannot be parsed logs a warning and falls back to real time. It never throws at class load.
6. Log a warning whenever the override is active and keep it out of production configuration.

```java
Clock system = Clock.system(platformZone);   // platformZone = defaultZoneId, not the JVM zone
Duration offset = Duration.between(Instant.now(), fake.atZone(system.getZone()).toInstant());
Clock business = Clock.offset(system, offset);   // advancing, not frozen
LocalDate today = LocalDate.now(business);
```

[6456] TOOLRESULT: - On by default in RDBMS (`rdbmsDaoOptimisticLockEnabled = true`). Insert sets version 1; each write increments.
- Check in `UpdatePayloadDaoProcessor` ONLY when payload has `__version`; mismatch → `IllegalArgumentException("Outdated instance to update")`. Omitting `__version` silently disables the guard.
- SQL: `VERSION` predicate only on meta UPDATE (`WHERE ID = :id AND VERSION = :__version`). Stale → no rows → "There is illegal state, no records updated".
- Clients echo `__version`; custom update ops rebuilding payloads must carry current version.
[6457] TOOLRESULT: 
- Optimistic locking is **on by default** in the RDBMS runtime (`rdbmsDaoOptimisticLockEnabled = true`). Inserts set the version to 1; each write increments it.
- The check happens in `UpdatePayloadDaoProcessor` and **only when the update payload contains `__version`**: it must equal the stored version, otherwise `IllegalArgumentException("Outdated instance to update")`. If `__version` is absent, no check is performed, so omitting it silently disables the guard.
- At SQL level the `VERSION` predicate is added only to the meta `UPDATE` (`WHERE ID = :id AND VERSION = :__version`), not to the attribute `UPDATE`. A stale version makes the statement update no rows, and the executor fails with "There is illegal state, no records updated".
- Clients must echo `__version` back and handle the stale-update error. Custom update operations that rebuild payloads must carry the current version.
[6458] TOOLRESULT: `update(entity)` and `update(entity, mask)` write only the attributes and relations that are **present in the entity's payload**. A field that was not loaded and not set is absent, so it is left unchanged in the database; the platform does not null it. The `mask` only selects what the returned entity contains (it is used for the read-back after the write), it does not limit what is written. `update` requires the identifier; defaults are applied only to payloads without one, so an update does not reset fields to model defaults.

To send only the changed fields, set just those fields on an entity that was not loaded in full:

```java
// Entity carrying its identifier and only the fields to change...
entity.setStatus(newStatus);
entity.setLastModified(LocalDateTime.now());

// The mask shapes the returned entity (read-back), not what is written
entityDao.update(
    entity,
    EntityMask.entityMask()
        .withStatus()
        .withLastModified()
);
```

[6462] CALL kb_get: {"path": "best-practices/backend/safe-failure-pattern.md", "section": "Practice > Platform base: what an exception costs you"}
[6463] TOOLRESULT: 
- Declare modeled faults in the model (`<faults>` in ESM, `throws` in JSL). Without it the SDK interface lacks `throws` and the build fails with `not compatible with throws clause`. See [Error Handling Guide](error-handling-guide.md#modeled-faults).
- Interceptors and write guards do NOT fire for custom (SDK-implemented) or script operations. CRUD on a mapped transfer object IS intercepted. Put permission checks inside each `*CustomImplementation`. See [Interceptors](interceptors.md#which-operations-are-intercepted).
- Transactions: one transaction per custom op. A thrown exception rolls back all writes. A returned fault also rolls back. A non-`stateful` operation is always rolled back, so an op that writes data must be `stateful`. A caught DAO exception does not roll back by itself (no rollback-only flag; the dispatcher commits a normal return); PostgreSQL then aborts the whole transaction, HSQLDB drops only the failed statement. Rethrow or return a fault to roll back.
- Optional input getters return `null` (not `Optional.empty()`) when the key is absent; `Optional` only when present. Null-check before `.orElse(...)`.
- Mapping: `<Op>CustomImplementation` implements `...operation.<model>.<path>.<Op>`; the interface path names the model operation.
- Stateful flag: a bound op keeps parameters compatible with every inherited op of the same name. Implementing an inherited abstract op must be `stateful`; the flag must match every overload that has an implementation.
- A fully im
[6464] TOOLRESULT: 
- Synchronous interceptors run inside the operation's transaction. An exception from `preCall` or `postCall` rolls back the DAO write of the operation, including writes of earlier interceptors ([ordering and combining interceptors](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#ordering-and-combining-several-interceptors)). A secondary interceptor that must not break the write has to catch its own failures.
- A raw `RuntimeException` leaving an interceptor is wrapped by the dispatcher as `InternalServerException` and the client sees a generic 500. A business refusal must be an `InterceptorCallBusinessException` ([throwing a business error from an interceptor](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#throwing-a-business-error-from-an-interceptor)).
- An async interceptor runs outside the transaction and its exceptions are lost, so it needs no catch for the transaction's sake, but should still log ([interceptor contract](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#the-interceptor-contract)).
- A custom operation runs in one transaction, so its DAO writes commit or roll back together when it throws ([custom operations](../../agent-docs/backend/custom-operations.md#rules-for-custom-operations)). A call that joins an existing transaction is not rolled back by itself ([platform behavior reference](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#transaction-rollback)). [6492] CALL kb_get: {"path": "model-blueprints/external-api-adapter-provider-pattern/backend.md", "section": "Backend guidance > The factory"}
[6493] TOOLRESULT: 
Keep the three layers in separate modules, so the API module has no dependency on any platform SDK:

- An API module: the interface and its data objects, packaged as a standalone bundle with no model dependency.
- One adapter module per platform: it depends on the API module and on that platform's SDK. Its implementation is a plain object.
- The common module: the provider interface and its factory implementation, which is the only component.
- The application's custom operations and services, which reference the provider.
[6494] TOOLRESULT: 
- The provider is a component in the common module. It injects the data-access objects (DAOs) it needs and the application's file store service for credential files.
- The lookup method does three things: load the entity, read its platform, load the platform's credentials. Then it constructs the adapter and returns it.
- Dispatch on the platform with one branch per platform. An unknown or not-yet-implemented platform fails with a business fault that carries an error code and a localized message.

lab() { run $J update "$1" -s "label=$2" --brief; }
lb() { to=$1; f=$2; l=$3; for x in Table View_Edit Form; do lab webshop::$to::${to}_$x::$f "$l"; done; }
nohid() { to=$1; shift; for f in "$@"; do for x in Table View_Edit Form; do run $J delete webshop::$to::${to}_$x::$f --no-check; done; done; }
# tbl TO relation "Title" col1,col2 : relation of the TO view shown as a table with columns
tbl() { to=$1; r=$2; title=$3; cols=$4; V=webshop::$to::${to}_View_Edit; run $J update $V --add feature --feature $r; run $J update $V::$r -s representationComponent=table; run $J update $V::$r --add feature --feature $cols --as column; lab $V::$r "$title"; }
act() { to=$1; a=$2; l=$3; hid=$4; V=webshop::$to::${to}_View_Edit; run $J update $V::actions --add feature --feature $a; lab $V::actions::$a "$l"; [ -n "$hid" ] && run $J update $V::actions::$a -s hiddenBy=webshop::$to.$hid; }
ract() { to=$1; r=$2; a=$3; l=$4; V=webshop::$to::${to}_View_Edit; run $J update $V::$r --add feature --feature $a --as rowAction; lab $V::$r::$a "$l"; }
inlab() { n=$1; title=$2; shift 2; for x in View_Edit Form; do lab webshop::$n::${n}_$x "$title"; while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do lab webshop::$n::${n}_$x::$1 "$2"; shift 2; done; set -- "${ARGS[@]}"; done; }

[6544] TOOLRESULT: - Adding a stored member to a fresh/new DB: no manual step; generated Liquibase changelog (`application/model/target/generated-resources/model/<app>-liquibase_postgresql.changelog.xml`) already creates the column.
- Existing DB is NOT migrated automatically. Startup fails `There are RDBMS model changes which have not been applied to database`. Migrate or discard DB+volume. Never use `ignoreVersionCheck` to get past it.
- Manual schema-upgrade needed for persistent DB with data and for non-additive changes (renames, type changes, removals).
- Local persistent PostgreSQL: `./judo.sh schema-upgrade` (generates diff vs running DB and applies).
- `judo.sh` schema module path = `schema_dir` (default `model_dir`). Set `schema_dir=<dir>` in `judo.properties` or env, e.g. `schema_dir=application ./judo.sh schema-upgrade`.
- `DATABASE HAVE TO BE UPDATED!` logged at error level by `generate` = notice, not failure; build still `BUILD SUCCESS`; migration needed before new model runs on that DB.
- Migration REQUIRED when entity types change: add/remove/modify mapped entities; attributes on entities; attribute types; relationships; entity inheritance.
- NOT required for: transfer object types, actors/access points, UI layouts/forms, operations (unless they affect entity structure), validations, derived attributes. Reason: schema generated only from entity types.
- Module: `application/schema/`; CLI tool + Docker image; auto script generation; incremental any-to-any; manual SQL support; Post

[6558] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/operation.md", "section": "`operation` Package Reference > Behaviour Rules > OperationType Context Constraints > In TransferObjectType (unmapped)"}
[6558] CALL kb_get: {"path": "best-practices/model/factory-operation-with-prepopulation.md", "section": "Practice > A static operation with an unmapped input and a typed output"}
[6559] TOOLRESULT: 
| OperationType | Available | Notes |
|---------------|-----------|-------|
| `INSTANCE` | ✗ | No entity instance to operate on |
| `STATIC` | ✓ | Only static operations allowed |
| `INITIALIZER` | ✓ | Factory operations |
| `MAPPED` | ✗ | No entity to map from |

An unmapped transfer object accepts only `STATIC` operations (EVL `UnmappedTransferObjectTypeHasStaticOperations`). The ESM→PSM transformation creates each as an unbound operation placed in that transfer object's own PSM equivalent (rule `CreateInPlaceUnboundOperation`); a `STATIC` operation on an entity is instead placed in the entity's default transfer object. Any unmapped transfer object can therefore carry static operations, not only a `StaticOperations` container. *Verify:* how an actor reaches the operation (access or menu item) was not settled in source.

**Summary Constraints:**
- `operationType = ABSTRACT` → owner is `EntityType` AND `advanced mode = true`
- `operationType = MAPPED` → owner is `TransferObjectType` AND `mapping` is NOT null
- `operationType = INSTANCE` → owner is `EntityType` OR (owner is `TransferObjectType` AND `mapping` is NOT null)
[6560] TOOLRESULT: 
- The factory is a static operation on a transfer object. Its input is an unmapped transfer object with the creation parameters; its output is the created entity's transfer type. Operation types, including `INITIALIZER` (a static operation without input), are described in the [operation reference](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/operation.md).
- The body can be a JCL script or custom Java ([JCL scripts](../../agent-docs/model/jcl-operation-scripts.md)). In JCL, `new Type(...)` must list every required attribute and relation, and reference sets are extended with `+=`. The `mutable` operator turns an input relation into an assignable reference.
- The body resolves the current user (see [[actor-context-variable-lookup]] for the `!getVariable("ACTOR", ...)` claim lookup), creates the parent, creates the children in a loop or explicitly, attaches them to the composition and returns the parent.
- Children belong to the parent by composition ([[composition-ownership]]), so the whole graph is created in one call and removed together.

[6584] TOOLRESULT: 
**Job component:**
- Expose the job as a `Runnable` service. Set the component to require its configuration, so the job starts only when its cron expression is provided.
- Turn off overlapping runs of the same job with the scheduler's concurrency property.
- Use a typed configuration definition for each tunable value (cron, thresholds, batch size).

```java
@Component(immediate = true, configurationPid = "example.job.cleanup",
           configurationPolicy = ConfigurationPolicy.REQUIRE,
           property = { "scheduler.concurrent:Boolean=false" },
           service = Runnable.class)
@Designate(ocd = CleanupJobConfig.class)
public class StaleRecordCleanupJob implements Runnable {

    @Reference
    private StaleRecordService staleRecordService; // example project code

    @Override
    public void run() {
        try {
            staleRecordService.purgeStaleRecords();
        } catch (Exception e) {
            LOG.error("Cleanup job failed", e); // never let the scheduler see the exception
        }
    }
}
```

`StaleRecordService` and the class names are example project code, not platform API.

**Business logic:**
- The service holds the work and reads the DAOs of the generated SDK through `@Reference`.
- The service handles each item on its own. A bad record is logged and skipped, so it does not stop the batch.
- Because a job is not an operation, interceptors do not run for the work it does (platform: interceptors fire only on built-in operations). If the same ru

[6590] TOOLRESULT: 
The platform has no scheduler: `judo-services-job` only runs the initializer operations at startup, and no Quartz or scheduling component exists in the runtime. Periodic work is application code (for example an OSGi component with its own executor).

- A scheduler thread has no request context: no principal and no `ACTOR`.
- To get the platform's transaction and operation handling, call a modeled operation through the `Dispatcher` (`callOperation`) instead of using the DAO directly; script and custom operations run in their own transaction (see Initializer Operations).
- Do not rely on `ACTOR`-based access or variables in such a call: the exchange carries no actor unless you put one in.
[6591] TOOLRESULT: 
A background job is an OSGi component that runs on a schedule (see [[osgi-scheduled-background-jobs]]). Scheduled execution happens outside the request-scoped transaction context, so database work in the job needs its own context (see the Quartz scheduled job practice). It calls the same transition service as the operations, so an activity finished by the job follows the same rules as one finished by a user.

- A scheduler activator component (example code `SingleInstanceSchedulerActivator`) requires its configuration PID (`configurationPolicy = REQUIRE`), starts Quartz with an in-memory job store and a small thread pool, and registers each job with a cron trigger. The RDBMS variant is optional: it sets the `org.apache.karaf.scheduler.quartz` PID and adds a JDBC job store; a shipped setup can use the in-memory single-instance mode instead:

```java
// Example code: not a platform type
@Component(configurationPid = { "schedulerConfig" },
           configurationPolicy = ConfigurationPolicy.REQUIRE, immediate = true)
public class SingleInstanceSchedulerActivator {
    // RAMJobStore, 4 threads
    void register(Scheduler scheduler, JobDetail job) throws SchedulerException {
        scheduler.scheduleJob(job, TriggerBuilder.newTrigger()
            .withSchedule(CronScheduleBuilder.cronSchedule("0 0 13 * * ?")).build());
    }
}
```

- Each job manages its own `UserTransaction`: begin, do the work, commit, roll back on failure.

[6596] TOOLRESULT: 
- A job is an ordinary OSGi component, so the Declarative Services rules apply ([OSGi component registration](../../agent-docs/backend/custom-operations.md#osgi-component-registration-pattern)); the scheduler runs in its own bundle ([[osgi-karaf-bundle-architecture]]).
- Request-scoped context is absent. Code on other threads runs outside the operation's transaction and without context variables ([interceptor contract](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#the-interceptor-contract)), so a job cannot use the `ACTOR` variable of a logged-in user. The docs do not describe the transaction and variable context of a plain scheduler thread; the practice below manages the transaction itself.
- Interceptors fire only for the built-in behaviour operations, and custom operations never hit them ([which operations are intercepted](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#which-operations-are-intercepted)). The docs do not mention scheduler threads; a job writes through DAOs, so treat denormalization or validation interceptors as not applying and repeat that logic in the job's service if needed.

- Admin or reference-data actors: keep fully generated ([[admin-default-generated-ui]]) and improve the experience in the model.

// ---- cart and orders --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

async function fillCart(page: Page, product: string, quantity: number) {
  await backToShop(page);
  await addFromDetails(page, product, quantity);
  await expect(page.getByText('Operation executed successfully')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
}

async function checkout(page: Page, po?: string): Promise<string | null> {
  await openCart(page);
  await btn(page, /Check out/).click();
  const d = page.getByRole('dialog');
  await expect(d).toBeVisible();
  await typeInto(page, d, /Shipping address/, 'Lake Road 9, 5003 Bergen, Norway');
  if (po) await typeInto(page, d, /Purchase order number/, po);
  await d.getByRole('button', { name: /(Ok|Submit)$/ }).click();
  const number = page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Number/ });
  const ok = await number.waitFor({ timeout: 20000 }).then(() => true).catch(() => false);
  if (!ok) return null;
  await expect(number).toHaveValue(/^WS-\d{4}-\d{6}$/, { timeout: 15000 });
  return number.inputValue();
}

const status = (page: Page) => page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Status/ });

  await clickMenu(page, 'My orders');

  await expect(page.getByRole('row').nth(1)).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  if (!(await cell.isVisible())) {
    await page.getByRole('columnheader', { name: /Ordered/

// Level 4: order lifecycle (F25-F27), product switches (F28, F29), conditional tracking number (F30), history (F31),
// 30 day order list (F32), plus the two shell fixes of round 9 (profile name, menu order).
// Every test signs up its own customer. Real user actions only; page.goto only for the app start URLs.

const rows = (page: Page) => page.locator('div[role="row"][aria-selected]');
const statusOf = (page: Page) => page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Status/ });
const numberOf = (page: Page) => page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Number/ });
const btn = (page: Page, name: string) => page.getByRole('button', { name });

async function placeOrder(page: Page, product = 'Day pack', quantity = 1): Promise<string> {
  if (quantity === 1) await addFromCard(page, product);
  else await addFromDetails(page, product, quantity);
  await openCart(page);
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: /Check out/ }).click();
  const d = page.getByRole('dialog');
  await expect(d).toBeVisible();
  await typeInto(page, d, /Shipping address/, 'Lake Road 9, 5003 Bergen, Norway');
  await d.getByRole('button', { name: /(Ok|Submit)$/ }).click();
  await expect(numberOf(page)).toHaveValue(/^WS-\d{4}-\d{6}$/, { timeout: 20000 });
  return numberOf(page).inputValue();
}

[6925] TOOLRESULT: 
`preCall` and `postCall` declare `throws InterceptorCallBusinessException` (package `hu.blackbelt.judo.runtime.core.dispatcher.behaviours`, a subclass of the platform `BusinessException`). It is the **only** business-level exception an interceptor may throw:

- A modeled fault's generated `<Fault>Exception` is checked and cannot be thrown from an interceptor; catch it from the service you called and rewrap it.
- A raw `RuntimeException` (or `IllegalStateException`) is wrapped by the dispatcher as `InternalServerException` and the client sees a generic 500.
- Interceptors do not handle business problems on their own; they refuse the call by wrapping the problem.

```java
throw new InterceptorCallBusinessException(
        "<app>.services.AlreadyShipped",                 // type: FQN of a modeled fault transfer object
        "ALREADY_SHIPPED",                               // errorCode
        Map.of("message", "The order was already shipped"), // details; never null
        null,                                            // cause (optional)
        null);                                           // locale: replaced by the dispatcher's locale
```

What happens next: the behaviour call catches the exception and returns a `_fault` payload; `DefaultDispatcher` turns it into a `BusinessException`; REST answers **422** with header `X-Fault: <type>` and body `{ "<type>": {details without "_"-prefixed keys and nulls}, "code": "<errorCode>" }`. Because interceptors run inside the o
[6926] TOOLRESULT: 
A business error arrives as HTTP 422 with an `X-Fault` header. The response body is an object with two keys: the fault's type name (holding the business error's `message`) and a `code` key. The business error's `message` is the text to show. By default the generated handler takes only the first key of the body; the server sorts the keys, so that is usually `code` rather than the fault name, and the handler opens the fault dialog for it, which shows every detail as a key and value; labels come from translation keys `faults.<fault name>.<attribute>` and fall back to the raw attribute name. Common practice: an error hook for the 422 status reads the message instead and shows it as a snack. See [[error-handler-interceptor-customization]].

[6951] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md", "section": "Operation Call Interceptors Guide > Overview > Ordering and Combining Several Interceptors"}
[6952] TOOLRESULT: 
All interceptors registered for an operation are called in **registration order** for `preCall` **and** for `postCall` (`postCall` is not reversed). Each `preCall` receives the original input payload, so interceptors see each other's in-place mutations of that payload; the objects they return are accumulated and the last non-null result wins. `postCall` behaves the same with respect to the return payload.

All synchronous interceptors run inside the transaction of the operation. An exception from `preCall` or `postCall` therefore rolls back the DAO write of the operation, including writes done by earlier interceptors.

async function saveView(page: Page) {
  await Promise.all([
    page.waitForResponse((r) => r.url().includes('/api/webshop/Admin') && r.request().method() !== 'GET'),
    page.getByRole('button', { name: /Update/ }).click(),
  ]);
  await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: /Update/ })).toBeHidden();
}

async function openFirstRow(page: Page, menu: string) {
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: menu, exact: true }).first().click();
  await expect(rows(page).first()).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, rows(page).first().getByRole('gridcell').nth(1), /.*~get$/);
}

// F33: change one number of the (single) shop settings row
async function setSetting(admin: Admin, label: RegExp, value: string) {
  await openFirstRow(admin.page, 'Shop settings');
  await typeNumber(admin.page, admin.page, label, value);
  await saveView(admin.page);
  await btn(admin.page, 'Back').click();
}

async function checkout(page: Page, extra?: { giftCard?: string; points?: string }): Promise<string | null> {
  await openCart(page);
  await btn(page, /Check out/).click();
  const d = page.getByRole('dialog');
  await expect(d).toBeVisible();
  await typeInto(page, d, /Shipping address/, 'Lake Road 9, 5003 Bergen, Norway');
  if (extra?.giftCard) await typeInto(page, d, /^Gift card code/, extra.giftCard);
  await resetPoints(d, extra?.points);
  await d.getByRole('button', { name: /(Ok|Submit)$/ }).click();
  const number = field(page, /^Number/);
  const ok = await number.waitFor({ timeout: 20000 }).then(() => true).catch(() => false);
  if (!ok) return null;
  await expect(number).toHaveValue(/^WS-\d{4}-\d{6}$/, { timeout: 15000 });
  return number.inputValue();
}

[7021] TOOLRESULT: import { type JudoStored } from './JudoStored';

export const draftIdentifierPrefix = 'draft:';

export const applyStoredMembers = (result: JudoStored<any>, instance: JudoStored<any>): void => {
  if (typeof instance.__deleteable === 'boolean') {
    result.__deleteable = instance.__deleteable;
  }
  if (typeof instance.__updateable === 'boolean') {
    result.__updateable = instance.__updateable;
  }
  if (typeof instance.__selected === 'boolean') {
    result.__selected = instance.__selected;
  }
  if (typeof instance.__version === 'string') {
    result.__version = instance.__version;
  }
  if (typeof instance.__entityType === 'string') {
    result.__entityType = instance.__entityType;
  }
  if (typeof instance.__identifier === 'string' && !instance.__identifier.startsWith(draftIdentifierPrefix)) {
    result.__identifier = instance.__identifier;
  }
  if (typeof instance.__signedIdentifier === 'string') {
    result.__signedIdentifier = instance.__signedIdentifier;

/**
 * F75: two admins edit the same product, the second save must not overwrite the first silently.
 * The platform already refuses a stale save when the payload carries the version it was read with
 * (agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md "Optimistic Locking and `__version`"), but the refusal is a plain
 * "There is illegal state, no records updated" and reaches the client as a generic 500 with no text. This interceptor
 * compares the version of the payload with the stored one first and refuses with a business error (422, code CONFLICT,
 * a readable message in the fault dialog), the documented way to refuse from an interceptor
 * (agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md "Throwing a Business Error from an Interceptor"). A payload without a version is
